THIS IS FUNCTION: --> test_sum_squaresACEFILENAMEdef sum_squares(lst):
    result =[]
    for i in range(len(lst)):
        if i %3 == 0:
            result.append(lst[i]**2)
        elif i % 4 == 0 and i%3 != 0:
            result.append(lst[i]**3)
        else:
            result.append(lst[i])
    return sum(result) <--

def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not

def test_sum_squares_empty():
    assert sum_squares([]) == 0


def test_sum_squares_single_element():
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 9


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_3_and_4():
    # Indices 0,3,6,9,12 are multiples of 3; 4,8 are multiples of 4 but not 3.
    lst = list(range(13))  # 0 to 12
    assert sum_squares(lst) == 882


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_4_not_3():
    lst = [2, 2, 2, 2, 2]  # index 4 -> cube 8
    expected = 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 8
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_sum_squares_negatives():
    lst = [-1, -2, -3, -4]
    # index 0 square: 1, indices 1 & 2 unchanged, index 3 square: 16
    expected = 1 - 2 - 3 + 16
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected

FINAL ANSWER 1
THIS IS FUNCTION: --> test_sum_squaresACEFILENAMEdef sum_squares(lst):
    """
    Calculate the sum of a list where each element is transformed based on its index:
    - If the index is a multiple of 3, the element is squared.
    - Else if the index is a multiple of 4, the element is cubed.
    - Otherwise, the element remains unchanged.

    Args:
        lst (list[int]): List of integers to process.

    Returns:
        int: Sum of the transformed elements.
    """
    total = 0
    for idx, val in enumerate(lst):
        if idx % 3 == 0:
            val = val ** 2
        elif idx % 4 == 0:
            val = val ** 3
        total += val
    return total <--

**Model's Predicted Answer:**
def sum_squares(lst):
    """
    Calculate the sum of a list where each element is transformed based on its index:
    - If the index is a multiple of 3, the element is squared.
    - Else if the index is a multiple of 4, the element is cubed.
    - Otherwise, the element remains unchanged.

    Args:
        lst (list[int]): List of integers to process.

    Returns:
        int: Sum of the transformed elements.
    """
    total = 0
    for idx, val in enumerate(lst):
        if idx % 3 == 0:
            val = val ** 2
        elif idx % 4 == 0:
            val = val ** 3
        total += val
    return total

def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not
    change the entries in the list whose indexes are not a multiple of 3 or 4. The function shall then return the sum of all entries.

    Examples:
    For lst = [1,2,3] the output should be 6
    For lst = []  the output should be 0
    For lst = [-1,-5,2,-1,-5]  the output should be -126
    """
. Entry point: sum_squares

**Context:**
def test_sum_squares_empty():
    assert sum_squares([]) == 0


def test_sum_squares_single_element():
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 9


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_3_and_4():
    # Indices 0,3,6,9,12 are multiples of 3; 4,8 are multiples of 4 but not 3.
    lst = list(range(13))  # 0 to 12
    assert sum_squares(lst) == 882


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_4_not_3():
    lst = [2, 2, 2, 2, 2]  # index 4 -> cube 8
    expected = 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 8
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_sum_squares_negatives():
    lst = [-1, -2, -3, -4]
    # index 0 square: 1, indices 1 & 2 unchanged, index 3 square: 16
    expected = 1 - 2 - 3 + 16
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected

def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a

**Model's Predicted Answer:**
def sum_squares(lst):
    """Return the sum of a transformed list.

    For each element in *lst*, if its index is a multiple of 3
    (including index 0) the element is squared; if its index is a
    multiple of 4 but **not** a multiple of 3 it is cubed.
    All other elements remain unchanged. The transformed values are
    summed and returned.
    """
    total = 0
    for idx, val in enumerate(lst):
        if idx % 3 == 0:
            total += val ** 2
        elif idx % 4 == 0:
            total += val ** 3
        else:
            total += val
    return total

def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not
    change the entries in the list whose indexes are not a multiple of 3 or 4. The function shall then return the sum of all entries.

    Examples:
    For lst = [1,2,3] the output should be 6
    For lst = []  the output should be 0
    For lst = [-1,-5,2,-1,-5]  the output should be -126
    """
. Entry point: sum_squares

**Context:**
def test_sum_squares_empty():
    assert sum_squares([]) == 0


def test_sum_squares_single_element():
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 9


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_3_and_4():
    # Indices 0,3,6,9,12 are multiples of 3; 4,8 are multiples of 4 but not 3.
    lst = list(range(13))  # 0 to 12
    assert sum_squares(lst) == 882


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_4_not_3():
    lst = [2, 2, 2, 2, 2]  # index 4 -> cube 8
    expected = 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 8
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_sum_squares_negatives():
    lst = [-1, -2, -3, -4]
    # index 0 square: 1, indices 1 & 2 unchanged, index 3 square: 16
    expected = 1 - 2 - 3 + 16
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected

def sum_squares(lst):
    """
    Return the sum of the list after squaring entries at indices that are multiples of 3,
    or cubing entries at indices that are multiples of 4 but not multiples of 3.
    """
    total = 0
    for i, val in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            total += val ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0 and i % 3 != 0:
            total += val ** 3
        else:
            total += val
    return total

**Question:**



def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not
    change the entries in the list whose indexes are not a multiple of 3 or 4. The function shall then return the sum of all entries.

    Examples:
    For lst = [1,2,3] the output should be 6
    For lst = []  the output should be 0
    For lst = [-1,-5,2,-1,-5]  the output should be -126
    """
. Entry point: sum_squares

**Context:**
def test_sum_squares_empty():
    assert sum_squares([]) == 0


def test_sum_squares_single_element():
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 9


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_3_and_4():
    # Indices 0,3,6,9,12 are multiples of 3; 4,8 are multiples of 4 but not 3.
    lst = list(range(13))  # 0 to 12
    assert sum_squares(lst) == 882


def test_sum_squares_multiple_of_4_not_3():
    lst = [2, 2, 2, 2, 2]  # index 4 -> cube 8
    expected = 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 8
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_sum_squares_negatives():
    lst = [-1, -2, -3, -4]
    # index 0 square: 1, indices 1 & 2 unchanged, index 3 square: 16
    expected = 1 - 2 - 3 + 16
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected

FINAL ANSWER 1
THIS IS FUNCTION: --> test_sum_squaresACEFILENAMEdef sum_squares(lst):
    """
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not
    change the entries in the list whose indexes are not a multiple of 3 or 4. The function shall then return the sum of all entries.

    Examples:
    For lst = [1,2,3] the output should be 6
    For lst = []  the output should be 0
    For lst = [-1,-5,2,-1,-5]  the output should be -126
    """
    total = 0
    for i, val in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            total += val ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0:
            total += val ** 3
        else:
            total += val
    return total <--

def test_index_0_is_squared():
    # Index 0 is a multiple of 3, so it must be squared
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 3 ** 2


def test_index_4_cubed_and_other_indices_unmodified():
    # Only index 4 is modified (cubed); others remain unchanged
    lst = [-2, -2, -2, -2, -2]
    expected = (-2) * 4 + (-2) ** 3  # four untouched elements + cubed element
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_index_12_squared_not_cubed():
    # Index 12 is a multiple of both 3 and 4; it should be squared only
    lst = list(range(13))  # indices 0-12
    # Manually compute expected sum
    expected = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            expected += v ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0:
            expected += v ** 3
        else:
            expected += v
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_mixed_indices_long_list():
    # A longer list to exercise several branches
    lst = list(range(20))
    result = sum_squares(lst)
    # Verify by recomputing manually
    manual = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            manual += v ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0:
            manual += v ** 3
        else:
            manual += v
    assert result == manual <--

def sum_squares(lst):
    """"
    This function will take a list of integers. For all entries in the list, the function shall square the integer entry if its index is a
    multiple of 3 and will cube the integer entry if its index is a multiple of 4 and not a multiple of 3. The function will not
    change the entries in the list whose indexes are not a multiple of 3 or 4. The function shall then return the sum of all entries.

    Examples:
    For lst = [1,2,3] the output should be 6
    For lst = []  the output should be 0
    For lst = [-1,-5,2,-1,-5]  the output should be -126

. Entry point: sum_squares

**Context:**
def test_example_cases():
    assert sum_squares([1, 2, 3]) == 6
    assert sum_squares([]) == 0
    assert sum_squares([-1, -5, 2, -1, -5]) == -126


def test_index_0_is_squared():
    # Index 0 is a multiple of 3, so it must be squared
    assert sum_squares([3]) == 3 ** 2


def test_index_4_cubed_and_other_indices_unmodified():
    # Only index 4 is modified (cubed); others remain unchanged
    lst = [-2, -2, -2, -2, -2]
    expected = (-2) * 4 + (-2) ** 3  # four untouched elements + cubed element
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_index_12_squared_not_cubed():
    # Index 12 is a multiple of both 3 and 4; it should be squared only
    lst = list(range(13))  # indices 0-12
    # Manually compute expected sum
    expected = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            expected += v ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0:
            expected += v ** 3
        else:
            expected += v
    assert sum_squares(lst) == expected


def test_mixed_indices_long_list():
    # A longer list to exercise several branches
    lst = list(range(20))
    result = sum_squares(lst)
    # Verify by recomputing manually
    manual = 0
    for i, v in enumerate(lst):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            manual += v ** 2
        elif i % 4 == 0:
            manual += v ** 3
        else:
            manual += v
    assert result == manual

- You will receive bulletpoints that are part of playbook that's used by the generator to answer the question.

def test_add_positive_numbers():
    assert add(2, 3) == 5
    assert add(5, 7) == 12

def test_add_with_zero():
    assert add(0, 0) == 0
    assert add(0, 5) == 5
    assert add(3, 0) == 3

def test_add_negative_numbers():
    assert add(-2, -3) == -5
    assert add(-5, -7) == -12

def test_add_mixed_sign_numbers():
    assert add(-2, 3) == 1
    assert add(5, -7) == -2

def test_add_large_numbers():
    large = 10 ** 12
    assert add(large, large) == 2 * large

def test_add_type_error():
    import pytest
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        add("2", 3)
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        add(2, "3")

THIS IS FUNCTION: --> test_cycpattern_checkACEFILENAMEdef cycpattern_check(a , b):
    l = len(b)
    pat = b + b
    for i in range(len(a) - l + 1):
        for j in range(l + 1):
            if a[i:i+l] == pat[j:j+l]:
                return True
    return False <--

FINAL ANSWER 1
THIS IS FUNCTION: --> test_cycpattern_checkACEFILENAMEdef cycpattern_check(a, b):
    """Return True if any rotation of ``b`` is a substring of ``a``.

    Cases handled:
    * Empty ``b`` – always True (empty string is a substring of any string).
    * ``b`` longer than ``a`` – impossible, return False.
    * ``b`` itself is a substring of ``a`` – return True.
    * Check every rotation of ``b`` for presence in ``a``.
    """
    if not b:  # empty ``b`` is always a substring
        return True
    if len(b) > len(a):
        return False
    if b in a:
        return True
    # generate rotations (excluding the original already checked)
    for i in range(1, len(b)):
        rotation = b[i:] + b[:i]
        if rotation in a:
            return True
    return False <--
